I really just sit in my room while everything slips away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It's just... it's always there. It feels like there’s no future for me beyond the apartment walls.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Like I said a minute ago, the pleasure is just completely gone. I really don't care about any of it anymore. Um... my art classes, the board-game nights, hiking... even soccer. I used to like them, but now I just don't have it in me to care. I'd really rather just sit in my room and let it all slip by.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, it started getting really bad... I mean, constant, around last summer. I keep having these thoughts that I don't want to wake up anymore. It's just too much. Actually, the other day, my friend came over and found me... I usually go most of the day without eating... food just tastes like cardboard to me now, so I don't really want it. Um... it's just been a constant struggle, honestly. I don't remember the last time I felt okay for more than a day or two, let alone a couple of months. It's just been this... this heavy feeling all year.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were so severe that you couldn't leave the house or take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's gotten pretty bad. in bed half the day, taking a shower or answering one message feels like I’ve already worked a full shift.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because... I just don't care about anything anymore. I used to do things, but now I have no interest in my art classes, or board-game nights, or hiking. I don't even care about getting back to the soccer team. I really just sit in my room while everything slips away.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling right in my chest. It's just... it's always there. It feels like there’s no future for me beyond the apartment walls.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: Like I said a minute ago, the pleasure is just completely gone. I really don't care about any of it anymore. Um... my art classes, the board-game nights, hiking... even soccer. I used to like them, but now I just don't have it in me to care. I'd really rather just sit in my room and let it all slip by.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Well, it started getting really bad... I mean, constant, around last summer. So I guess it's been about a year and four months now that I've felt like this every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep having these thoughts that I don't want to wake up anymore. It's just too much. Actually, the other day, my friend came over and found me... I was just sitting there, saying out loud that I couldn't keep living like this. So, yeah, the thoughts are definitely there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how you move throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really goes back and forth. Um... some days I just pace. I'll walk from the kitchen to the window, over and over, until my legs actually ache. But then other times, I just move so slowly. I just have no energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I've lost quite a bit of weight. I usually go most of the day without eating... food just tastes like cardboard to me now, so I don't really want it. It's gotten to the point where my jeans are so loose that I actually have to knot the waistband just to keep them on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt significantly better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um... it's just been a constant struggle, honestly. I don't remember the last time I felt okay for more than a day or two, let alone a couple of months. Honestly, my friend has been coming over to check on me because... well, I'm just not reliably eating or showering. She makes sure I'm actually keeping myself safe, because left to my own devices, I just... I don't do those things right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your symptoms were less severe, but still noticeable, and you were able to manage your daily life with some effort?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there was a stretch before the worst of this depression set in. I mean, constant, around last summer. So I guess it's been about a year and four months now that I've felt like this every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I mean, constant, around last summer. I keep having these thoughts that I don't want to wake up anymore. It's just too much. Actually, the other day, my friend came over and found me... I was just sitting there, saying out loud that I couldn't keep living like this. I usually go most of the day without eating... food just tastes like cardboard to me now, so I don't really want it. It's gotten to the point where my jeans are so loose that I actually have to knot the waistband just to keep them on.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt significantly better for at least a couple of months?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um... it's just been a constant struggle, honestly. I don't remember the last time I felt okay for more than a day or two, let alone a couple of months. It's just been this... this heavy feeling all year.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where your symptoms were so severe that you couldn't leave the house or take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's gotten pretty bad. I've completely stopped taking any freelance art-education work. I just can't manage it. And I don't go to community events or play soccer anymore, either. I barely leave the house. Honestly, my friend has been coming over to check on me because... well, I'm just not reliably eating or showering. She makes sure I'm actually keeping myself safe, because left to my own devices, I just... I don't do those things right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where your symptoms were less severe, but still noticeable, and you were able to manage your daily life with some effort?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there was a stretch before the worst of this depression set in. I was able to manage a bit better then. I could still cook for myself sometimes, and I'd reply to my friends when they checked in. But... even then, things weren't right. I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed if they dominate my thoughts most of the time. I just haven't kept track of it like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced the voices or feelings of being watched when you weren't feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes.
